I'm sorry if that makes me sound stubborn or ungrateful to my family, I just... I know what's happening. I hope it's alright that I'm being so honest about it with you?\n\nDoctor: Do other people in your family share this belief about your husband’s sister?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, my husband and the others, they... they mostly just tell me I'm reading too much into things. I know it probably makes me look like the odd one out, and I hate causing any friction, I really do. I try to keep it to myself mostly so I don't upset anyone. They just don't see what she's doing behind the scenes, I guess. Is... is it normal for families to see things so differently? I just want to make sure I'm explaining this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other strong beliefs like this about people trying to harm, control, or deceive you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. Um... I don't think so, anyway. I really try to get along with everyone and give people the benefit of the doubt, you know? I hate the idea of thinking badly about people. It's just... it's just this one situation with my sister-in-law because it's so clear to me. But no, I haven't had any other... well, I haven't felt like anyone else is doing anything like that. I hope that's a good thing? I just try to be a good person and see the good in others. I just want to make sure I'm explaining this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other strong beliefs like this about people trying to harm, control, or deceive you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I suppose I would try to listen, I really would. I want to be reasonable about this, I promise you that. But... oh, it's just so hard, doctor. 

I mean, I only came in today because... well, because of what she's been doing. She's been working her way into the family business, and I just know it's so she can get control of the money and push me completely out of all the decisions. And... well, my husband and others, they do try to smooth it over. They tell me I'm misunderstanding things, and I know they mean well, I really do. 

But I've been seeing this happen for more than a year now. I know what I've seen with my own eyes. So... even if they showed me what they thought was proof, I just... I don't think I could just ignore everything she's done. I'm not trying to be difficult or stubborn, I really aren't. I just have to protect my family, you know? 

I hope that doesn't make me sound awful or... or unreasonable. I really am trying my best to handle this the right way. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure you understand where I'm coming from.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, yes, absolutely. I think I mentioned that just a moment ago... I've always been very trusting. Maybe even too trusting sometimes, if I'm being perfectly honest. 

I've always believed that if you treat people with kindness and respect, they'll do the same for you. I never had any reason to doubt anyone before she started acting this way. I just... I really want you to know that this isn't how I normally am. I've always been the kind of person who welcomes new people with open arms. 

I hope it's okay that I'm repeating myself a little bit. I just want to be absolutely sure you know that I'm not a suspicious person by nature. Am I making sense? I really want to be helpful and make sure you're getting a clear picture of who I am.

Um... I don't think so, anyway. I really try to get along with everyone and give people the benefit of the doubt, you know? I hate the idea of thinking badly about people. It's just... it's just this one situation with my sister-in-law because it's so clear to me. But no, I haven't had any other... well, I haven't felt like anyone else is doing anything like that. I hope that's a good thing? I just try to be a good person and see the good in others. Does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your husband’s sister is able to read your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no! No, nothing like that. I mean, I know I might sound a little... well, a little focused on what she's doing with the money, but I definitely don't think she can read my mind or anything impossible like that. I'm not... I'm not crazy, I promise. It's just regular things she says and does that I see with my own eyes, you know? Just everyday things. I hope I haven't made it sound like I believe in magic or mind-reading or anything... I really just want to be sensible about all this. Am I making sense? I just want to make sure you know I'm thinking clearly.\n\nDoctor: If you were shown clear proof that your husband’s sister is not trying to control the money, would you believe it?\n\nPatient: Oh... well... I want to say yes, I really do. I'd love nothing more than to just find out I'm wrong and let it all go. It would make things so much easier for everybody, and I hate being the cause of any trouble. \n\nBut... um... I just don't know how there could be proof like that, you know? Because... well, the whole reason I came in is because she’s been working this family business so she can get control of the money and push me out of the decisions. And... I know folks try to smooth it over, they really do, but I know what I’ve been seeing for more than a year now. \n\nI'm sorry, I don't mean to sound stubborn or... or difficult. I just can't unsee what she's been doing, even if I really want to keep the peace. Does that make me sound awful? I promise I'm trying to be reasonable about it all, I just have to trust my own eyes. Is it okay that I feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that other people around you did not?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. Um... I've never had anything like that happen. I know sometimes people who are really sick might hear voices or see things, but I've never experienced anything like that at all. My hearing and eyesight are just fine, just the normal stuff for someone my age. I really hope you don't think I'm... well, that I'm losing touch with reality or anything. I'm just a normal person trying to figure out a difficult family situation. I haven't said anything that would make you think I'm seeing things, have I? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had long-standing difficulties with your relationships besides this situation with your husband’s sister?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my husband and I get along just fine, and... and I have a few friends from church that I see sometimes. I try to be a good neighbor, too. I really try to get along with everybody. \n\nI mean, sure, every family has its little disagreements, right? That's just normal. But I don't have a history of... of not getting along with people, if that's what you mean. I've always tried to be the kind of person who keeps the peace. I really hope I don't come across as someone who just goes around picking fights or... or causing trouble. I really am a friendly person, I promise. Does that answer what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief caused problems with your work, home life, or relationships?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, not really. I try very hard not to let it cause any real problems. I mean, I still keep up with all my usual routines around the house, and I go to my church activities just like always. Everything else is completely fine, I promise. \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest... I have skipped a few family get-togethers recently. Just... just to keep the peace, you know? I don't want to cause any awkwardness or make anyone uncomfortable. And I suppose I do keep certain plans and, you know, little money matters to myself now. Just so it doesn't get back to my husband's sister and cause a fuss. \n\nBut outside of that, everything is normal. I hope you don't think I'm letting this ruin things or... or that I'm behaving badly. I'm really just trying to be careful and keep things running smoothly for everyone. Am I explaining that okay? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your husband’s sister has any unusual powers or special abilities beyond normal people?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that at all. Um... she's just a regular person, just like you or me. I don't think she has any kind of... you know, magic or special powers or anything strange. \n\nI just think she's... well, maybe just very good at being sneaky and stirring up trouble. But just in a normal way, not anything supernatural. I really hope I didn't say anything earlier that made you think I believe in that sort of thing. I know how silly that would sound, and I promise I'm completely level-headed about all this. I'm just trying to be careful around her, that's all. \n\nDid I explain that right? I just want to make sure I'm not giving you the wrong impression of me, or making it sound like my imagination is running wild. I'm just trying to answer exactly what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that other people, besides your husband’s sister, are out to harm, deceive, or take advantage of you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, definitely not. It's really just her. I don't go around thinking everyone is out to get me or anything like that. I really try to see the best in people, I do. \n\nMost people are just... well, they're just good people trying to get by, aren't they? I trust my friends at church, and I trust my husband, of course. I've never had any reason to think anyone else is trying to trick me or take advantage. It's just this one specific situation with his sister that's... well, a little difficult. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm the sort of person who is always suspicious of everyone. I really am quite trusting most of the time. I wouldn't want you to think I'm being paranoid or anything silly like that. Is that what you were wondering? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt unusually excited or energetic for days at a time with little need for sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no. No, I've never had anything like that happen. I'm really a very... well, a very quiet, steady sort of person. \n\nI definitely need my sleep every night to function properly, just like anyone else. I don't think I could ever go days without resting, and I certainly don't get all worked up or... or overly energetic like you're describing. I just try to keep an even keel, you know? Keep everything calm and normal around the house. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? I wouldn't want you to think I'm bouncing off the walls or acting out of control or anything like that. I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking... did I explain that alright?\n\nDoctor: If your husband and others showed you clear, objective proof that your sister-in-law is not trying to control the money, would you be able to accept that?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I would try to listen, I really would. I want to be reasonable about this, I promise you that. But... oh, it's just so hard, doctor. \n\nI mean, I only came in today because... well, because of what she's been doing. She's been working her way into the family business, and I just know it's so she can get control of the money and push me completely out of all the decisions. And... well, my husband and others, they do try to smooth it over. They tell me I'm misunderstanding things, and I know they mean well, I really do. \n\nBut I've been seeing this happen for more than a year now. I know what I've seen with my own eyes. So... even if they showed me what they thought was proof, I just... I don't think I could just ignore everything she's done. I'm not trying to be difficult or stubborn, I really aren't. I just have to protect my family, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound awful or... or unreasonable. I really am trying my best to handle this the right way. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure you understand where I'm coming from.\n\nDoctor: Have you generally been a suspicious or distrustful person for most of your adult life, even before this situation with your sister-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I've never been like that, I promise. I've always been someone who takes people at their word, really. \n\nBefore all this started with his sister, I... well, I never really gave a second thought to whether people were being honest with me. I've always just assumed people are basically good and mean well. I've never been one to hold grudges or go looking for hidden meanings in what people say or do. I try to be friendly with everyone, I really do. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm some sort of paranoid person who's always looking over her shoulder. That's not me at all. I just... I want you to know I've always been very trusting and open with people. Does that sound like what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea about what kind of person I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of several days or longer when your mood was unusually high or irritable?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I... I really pride myself on being easy to get along with, so I definitely don't get all irritable or snappy for days on end. I just want to make sure I'm being helpful and giving you exactly what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Before all of this with your sister-in-law started, did you generally trust people you met?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, absolutely.
